Ek aur deck Robert Frost ki shayri (poetry) ki ek kitab se liye gaye lafzon ka hai: *breast* (seena), *flight* (udaan), *wings* (par), *heaven* (jannat), *light* (roshni), *undergrowth* (jhariyan), *winter* (sardiyan). Baad ki classes mein, wo kai cards (multiple cards) chunte hain, waqt ko badha kar do minute kar dete hain, aur students ko in cards ke mail (combination) se drawing banane ka kehte hain. Ek waqt mein do cards, phir teen, chaar, paanch.

Hristoff assignment ki wajah batate hain:

Yeh hint (prompt) unki creativity (takhleequi salahiyat) ko baahar nikaalti hai (releases). Yeh unki creativity ko jagane (release) ka bahut aasaan (simple) tarika hai. Yeh students ko bohot prabhavit (resonates) karta hai. Yeh khali safay (blank page) ke pressure (dabao) ko door karne ka ek tarika hai, taaki students yeh samajh (realize) sakein ki koi bhi cheez drawing shuru karne ke liye ek hint ban sakti hai. Aur phir ek baar jab aap drawing shuru kar dete hain, toh jaadu (magic) shuru ho jata hai—aap isme apni kahani le aate hain. Ise padhana bahut mazedar (fun) hota hai. Main poori tarah se doob jata hoon (get into a zone). Main poori tarah dhyan mein kho jata hoon. Yeh waqai (amazing) hai ki kis tarah bas hints padh kar aur timer ke sath chal kar, aur phir ek sath do cards par jakar, ek sath teen cards par jakar [wo drawing poori karte hain]. Aur phir jab aap paanch cards par pahunchte hain, drawing chhod dain. Achanak wo choti (mini) kavitayen (poems) ban jate hain. Unhe jaldi (faster) draw karna padta hai kyunki yeh ek bohot hi spontaneous (fitrati/azaad) qism ki drawing hoti hai. Khas taur par us student ke liye jo apni drawing ko lekar thoda insecure (be-yakeeni) mehsus karta hai ya jiske dimaag mein pehle se hi yeh baat basi hui hai (preconceived ideas) ki ek achi drawing kya hoti hai. Main unhe itna waqt (enough time) nahi dena chahta ki wo pareshan (uptight) ho jayein; main bas unhe react karne ke liye kafi waqt dena chahta hoon. Aur yeh unme ek khas kism ka confidence (aetmaad) paida karta hai. Yeh discipline (nazm-o-zabt) hai; bas khud ko iske hawale kar do (commit yourself). Yeh ek aisi cheez hai jo koi (apne sath) le ja sakta hai. Main koi life coach (zindagi sawarne wala) nahi hoon, lekin yeh kisi life coach ki baat jaisa lagta hai. Agar aap khud ko kisi bhi kaam mein laga dete hain, toh aap usme behtar ho jayenge. Zindagi mein aage badhna (grow), kisi cheez mein behtar hona eham (important) hai.

Kyun students ko saat narratives (kahaaniyon) ki list ya paanch novels (kahaaniyon ki kitabon) ki list mein se chunkar kaam karne ka kaha jata hai? Kyunki agar aisa na kiya jaye, toh students content (mawaad/kahaani) ke baare mein sochne mein bohot zyada waqt zaya (spend too much time) kar denge, is bajaye ke wo us content ko tasweer ki surat (visual representation) mein laane ka sochein. Is class mein unke liye yeh eham nahi hai ki wo bohot saare novels padhein aur wo chunein jo unhe pasand hai.

In constraints (rukawaton) ki ek aur wajah yeh hai ki yeh students ko sabse saaf (obvious) aur comfortable (aaraam de) cheez karne se rokti hain. Agar Pike kehta, "Ek narrative (kahaani) chuno aur usay illustrate (khaanaka/tasweer mein bayaan) karo," toh wo us event ki kahani chunte jisse wo pehle se wakif hain—shayad apni khud ki zindagi se. Agar Corcoran kehti, "Ek novel chuno," toh students shayad apni pasandeeda novel ya aisi novel chunte jo unhone haal hi mein padhi ho. Ya phir wo bohot saari novels padhne mein bohot sara waqt guzarte aur ek aisi novel dhoondhne ki koshish karte jo unki assignment ki samajh se sabse zyada milti-julti ho.

School of Visual Arts (SVA) ke Richard Mehl apni advanced typography (type designing) class mein bhi aise hi assignments ka tarika (approach) apnate hain, jisme juniors aur seniors hotay hain. "Main unhe kaam karne ke liye content (mawaad) deta hoon. Kyunki warna wo do ya teen hafte content sochne mein nikal denge. Aap nahi chahte ki wo yeh sochein aur apna qeemti waqt (precious time) is baat ka faisla (deciding) karne mein barbad karein ki kya kiya jaye. Sirf is baat ka faisla karne mein hi do ya teen hafte lag sakte hain ki wo kya content istemal karna chahte hain. Lekin mera maqsad (purpose) skills sikhana hai. Letter spacing (harfon ke darmian jagah). Line spacing." Bahut se design assignments mein students se "Lorem ipsum" text—jo ke be-maani (meaningless) Latin shabd hain—ka istemal karne ko kaha jata hai taaki students ka dhyan design ke visual (dikhnay walay) elements se na hate.

Kabhi-kabhi, seekhne ka maqsad iske bilkul ulat (opposite) hota hai: students ko text par ghor (focus) karne ki zaroorat hoti hai. Amy Thompson, jo typeface design aur book design sikhati hain, ne mujhe bataya: "Yeh shayad ek text-based (text par mubni) project ho aur main chahti hoon ki wo sach mein imagery (tasweerat) banane ke liye text ka istemal karein, aur wo poochenge ki kya wo images (tasweeron) ka bhi istemal kar sakte hain, aur main inkaar kar doongi, kyunki yeh baad mein hoga." Assignment ki pabandiyan (constraints) seekhne ke umeed-kardah nateejon (desired learning outcome) ke hisab se badalti rehti hain.

Ek healthcare product ke ad campaign wale assignment ke liye, professor ne category aur products ki list pehle hi teh (assign) kar di thi; students ko apni marzi se kuch bhi chunne ka mauka nahi mila. Is baat se koi zyada farq nahi padta ki category healthcare products hai; jo tareeqa (process) wo seekhenge, wo kisi bhi product category par laagu kiya ja sakta hai. Lekin zaroori yeh hai ki aap unhe 'kuch bhi' (anything) karne ka kehne ki bajaey koi makhsoos cheez (something) dein. Wo explain karte hain: "Aapne in barray sochne ke dhang (bigger ways of thinking) sikhane ke maqsad (purpose) se unke options ko mehdood (constrained) kiya hai."

### **Kai Options (Possibilities) Tay Karein (Assign Multiple Possibilities)**

Tiffany Holmes (Ringling) ne ek drawing assignment ke baare mein bataya jo wo us waqt deti thin jab wo University of Michigan mein thin:

*Students mujh par bohot ghussa (mad) the kyunki maine unse kaha tha ki pure semester ke liye unhe ek aisa object (cheez) chunna tha jo unke hath se chhota aur quarter (sikke) se bada ho. Aur wahi unhe draw karna tha. Unhe ek hafte mein us object ki 5 drawing banani thi. Aur semester ke aakhir tak, wo is se bohot thak (bored) chuke the. Lekin unke paas be-misaal tor par dilchasp (incredibly compelling) sketchbooks the. Yeh dekhna ek ajeeb (cool) cheez thi, bas unki skills (facility) aur object ko draw karne (rendering) ka dikhnay wala irtaqa (visual growth).*

Art aur design ke assignments shuruat mein hi ek bohot achay idea (great idea) ki maang nahi karte kyunki behtareen ideas kaam karne ke process se ubhar kar aate hain. Bahut se assignments is baat ki maang karte hain ki students ek khas ginti (specific number) mein naye raaste ya ideas paida (generate) karein—jaise chaar, das, bees, ya yahan tak ki pachaas ideas aage badhne ke tareeqo par (how to proceed). Cheryl Wassenaar (WashU) ne mujhe bataya: "Yeh idea bahut zyada ideas sochne (generating a lot) aur jo aapke paas hai use dekhne ka hai, unme se kuch ko chunna jo faydemand (useful) lagen, aur phir us idea par aur ideas banana (generating more). Agar aap kisi cheez ko karne ke sirf teen tareeqe sochte hain, toh iska matlab hai ki aapka dimaag poori mehnat (flexing its muscles) nahi kar raha hai." Creativity par research karne wale ise *divergent thinking (alag-alag sochna)* kehte hain: Jitne zyada ideas soch sakte ho, socho, bhale hi unme se bahut se bekaar lag rahe hon. Is shuruaati marahil (early stage) mein is baat ki fikar na karo ki wo kitne achay hain; warna, tum apni poori dimaagi tawanai (mental energy) ek behtareen idea sochne mein laga doge.

**Assignments process ko shuru karne wale trigger points hain aur wahan se kuch kamaal (amazing) ho sakta hai. Theek hai na? Humein abhi nahi pata. Yeh sabse bewaqoofi wali cheez (dumbest thing) bhi ho sakti hai!**

Candida Alvarez, artist, SAIC

Douglas Dowd (illustration, WashU) apne chauthe saal ke illustration students ko sirf ek weekend mein 100 drawings banane ko kehte hain:

*Main unhe ek task deta hoon jahan unhe ek weekend mein 100 figure drawings banani hoti hain. Insaano ki drawings. Yeh pagal kar dene wala (maddening) problem hai, kyunki aapke paas jitni bhi tricks hain, wo ideas jo naturally (qudrati taur par) dimaag mein aate hain, wo sab 30 tak pahunchte-pahunchte khatam ho jate hain. Phir bhi 70 baaqi rehti hain. Yeh thaka dene wala (grueling) kaam hai. Aakhir mein, aap un sab ko (display ke liye) laga dete hain aur wahi aapka data set ban jata hai. Aur sawal yeh hota hai ki deewar par lage in 100 kamo mein se kaun se sabse dilchasp lag rahe hain? Kuch aisa ubhar (happens) aata hai jiska student ne kabhi socha (intended) nahi hota, siwae un mushkil halaaton (extreme circumstances) ke, jisse ek aesa rasta (opportunity) khulta hai jiska unhe tasawwur bhi nahi tha. Wo kuch aesa karte hain jo waqai mazedar (exciting) hota hai. Jis se wo faida utha sakte hain (capitalize on). Toh phir aap us daryaft (discovery) par ghaur karne ya uska faida uthane ke liye aese problems design karna shuru kar dete hain.*

Arny Nadler, WashU mein 1997 se padha rahe hain aur 2013 se 2018 tak undergraduate art program ke chair rahe hain. Wo ideas paida (idea generation) karne ko *serious play (sanjeda khel)* ki tarah dekhte hain:

*Ek acha artist yeh puchega, "Kya sahi (working) ho raha hai aur kya galat (not working)? Inmein se kaunsa aisa kaam kar raha hai jo tumhe dilchasp (interesting) lag raha hai?" Lekin aap ek student ko yeh karna kaise sikhayenge? Mujhe lagta hai ki aap ek aesa curriculum (nisaab) design karte hain jahan wo sach mein isey tajurba (experience) kar saken. Jahan unhe darjano (dozen) sketches banane hon. Jab ek student darjano sketches banata hai, aur phir dusre student ko un sketches ko milana (combine) hota hai, ek tarah se chhan-been (pick apart) karni hoti hai ki kaun se achay hain aur bure ko nikalna (throw out) hota hai, aur achay ideas ko pakad ke rakhna (hold on) hota hai. Jitna zyada wo yeh karte hain, utna hi zyada wo is sanjeda khel (serious play) ko tajurba karte hain, jisko bayaan (describe) karne ka sabse behtar tareeqa shayad yahi hai. Main sach mein manta hoon ki har cheez mein ek dilchasp khel-kood wali sifat (playful quality) hoti hai. Ek tarah ka curious (utsuk) aur playful (khel khel mein) sawal karna (inquiry) hota hai. Wahi waqt hota hai jab sach mein, sach mein kamaal ki (cool) cheezein samne aati hain. Mera man-na hai ki ek khasi tadad (decent quantity) mein sketches banwana zaroori hona chahiye. Yeh unhe pehle, dusre, teesre idea se aage, naye imkanaat (possibilities) ki taraf badhne mein madad karta hai. Main unhe batata hoon ki ek aesa waqt hona chahiye jab wo sketches par bina kisi judgement (faisle) ke kaam kar rahe hon. Tumhe is kaam ko bas jaldi jaldi bahar nikalna hai (pooping this stuff out). In par bohot zyada mat socho (overthink); bus mukhtalif (different) ashkalo (forms) ka ambar (bunch) paida (generate) karo.*

### **Figures & Stories (Insaani shakal aur Kahaniyan)**

**Jeff Pike, Pictures for Communication, Spring 2011, Washington University in St. Louis**

Aap ek aisi two-color (do rangu) illustration banayenge jo niche di gayi kahanion (stories) mein se kisi ek ko saaf-saaf bayan kare. In mumkin (potential) kahanion (narratives) mein se sirf ek chunein.

**The Lesson (Sabaq) The Escape (Faraar/Bhagna) The Revenge (Badla) The Negotiation (Samjhota/Baat-cheet) The Breakup (Rishta Tootna) The Injury (Chot) The Stake Out (Nazar Rakhna) The Dance (Raqs) The Triumph (Jeet) The Advance (Aage Badhna) The Envious (Hasad/Jalan) The Tragedy (Haadsa) The Contest (Mukabla) The Outrage (Shadeed Gussa)**

Pehle, humne Pike ki class ke dusre project, *A Short Comic Event*, ke baare mein padha tha, jise John Hendrix ne design kiya tha. Semester ka chautha (4th) aur aakhiri project, *Figures & Stories*, students ko Pike ki taraf se di gayi narratives (kahanion) ki ek list mein se kisi ek ko chun kar ek narrative editorial piece illustrate (khenchna/tasveer mein bayan) karne ka kehta hai (dekhein aglay safhay (page) ka ooper hissa). Main ek class mein gaya jahan students pinup critiques (deewar par laga kar jaiza lena) kar rahe the. Pehle student ne "The Escape" narrative chuna tha. Usne deewar par apne das (10) chote draft sketches, jinhe *thumbnails* kaha jata hai, lagaye the. Pinup ke baad, student ko in das mein se ek chunna tha jiske baad wo is assignment ke agle hisse par kaam karta. Student ne yeh kehna shuru kiya, "Mujhe inmein se koi bhi pasand nahi hai. Yeh sab ajeeb tarah se (kind of, like), boring aur baghair kisi asar (uninspired) ke hain." Yeh Pike ke liye poori class ke samne ek zaroori (important) baat rakhne ka ek badiya mauka tha:

*Jab aap pinup karte hain aur aapko is khayal ke sath shuruat karni padhti hai ki "yeh sabhi bekar (suck) hain," toh aap kya karte hain? Aap isse bahar kaise nikalte hain? Aapko us point se aage badhne ke liye ek plan (strategy) chahiye. Thumbnails sirf shuruat hain. Hum hamesha koshish karte hain ki bahut saare thumbnails banayein, taaki hamare paas bahut saare potential (mumkin) ideas hon, jisse ek idea se doosre ideas janam lein (suggest other ideas). Yahan tak ki bure ideas (bad ideas) se bhi behtar ideas (better ideas) nikal sakte hain. Thumbnail stage par, yeh sab mumkin sone ki khan (potential goldmines) ho sakte hain, ya nahi. Aap escape (bhagne) ke topic (theme) ke sath jude reh sakte hain, isme chhote janwar shamil karke Bee Horror Show bana sakte hain, jaise Peter Rabbit Farmer McGregor se bach ke bhag raha ho. Wo un par rocket-propelled grenades chala raha ho sakta hai. Mera matlab hai, aap isey kisi bhi cheez mein badal (turn) sakte hain jo aap chahein. Lekin is stage par problem ka ek hissa apnay hi ideas ko baar-baar parkhna (awful self-editing) hai. Apnay ideas ko baar-baar mat parkho (Don't self-edit). Is muqaam par (At this stage of the game), khud ko judge (self-editing) karna ek bahut, bahut, bahut buri cheez hai.*

Thodi behes (discussion) ke baad, is student ne us thumbnail ko chuna jisme do log ghod-sawari karke ek azdaha (giant monster) se bhag rahe the. Aakhri lamhe mein (At the last minute), usay us azdahe ko pachaas (50) foot ooche Peter Rabbit mein badalne ka idea aaya jiska sketch usne ek alag thumbnail mein banaya tha.

Phir class ne doosre student ke banaye 10 thumbnails ko dekha, jisne "The Envious (Hasad)" ko chuna tha. Uske thumbnail sketches mein shamil the:

* Do judwa (twins) aapas mein behan bhai ki hasad (sibling rivalry) kar rahe hain
* Teen bhai behen (siblings), jismein bada aur chhota mil kar beech walay bacche (middle child) ke khilaf team bana rahe hain
* Ek tasveer jismein ek aurat ko shadi ke liye poocha (proposed) ja raha hai, aur peechay khari ek aur aurat ussi aadmi se be-tahasha mohabbat karti hai
* Ek patli (skinny) ladki sheeshe mein khud ko dekh rahi hai aur ek moti (bigger) ladki usay dekh rahi hai
* Ek ladki apne locker ke paas khadi hai, jahan ek khubsurat ladka (cute guy) uski taraf haath hila (waving) raha hai, lekin asal mein wo uske peechay khadi kisi aur ladki ko haath hila raha hai
* Ek aesa shakhs jiske paas bahut zyada paisa hai, jo sadak par chal raha hai
* Ek student jo ek teacher ko pasand (crush) karti hai aur phir teacher ki patni classroom mein dakhil hoti hai

Thodi baat-cheet ke baad, usne behan bhai ki hasad (sibling rivalry) wala theme chuna. Pike ne kaha, "Aaj, tum isay gehrai se (in depth) explore (khoj/analyze) karoge. Behan bhai ki hasad par 10 ideas."

Teesre student ne "The Revenge" chuna tha. Uske pasandeeda sketch ka idea uss waqt se juda tha jab uska ek dost concert mein kisi jhagde mein pad gaya tha. Lekin jaise hi wo apna sketch bayaan karne laga, yeh saaf hone laga ki story intahai pechida (too complex) hai, jo ek aasan do-rang (two-color) wali illustration mein puri nahi aa sakti:

*Mera ek dost concert mein gaya. High school ke ek ladke ne socha ke usne uska wallet chura liya hai toh us high school kid ne mere dost ke muh par punch maara, phir mere dost ne usay gaali di aur wahan se bhaag gaya. Ye sab andar chal raha tha, phir wo kisi na kisi tarah (somehow) bahar footpath (sidewalk) par pohnch gaye, wahan meri ek dusri dost apni car me aai aur boli, "Jaldi! Andar baitho!"*

Student ne qissay (episode) ki thodi aur details dena shuru kar di; kahani aur bhi zyada confusing (uljhan mein dalne wali) hoti ja rahi thi. Pike ne jawab (responded) diya:

*Ye ek pechida satah (complicated scenario) hai. Tumhein ek waqay (moment) par focus karne ki zaroorat hai. Yeh ghalatfehmi (mistaken identity) hai. Kya hum punch dekhne wale hain ya punch se pehle ka waqt? Kya hum gaadi aate dekhenge? Kya sabse zaroori (important) hai? Kon si information jan-na zaroori hai? Kon si rasta (direction) sabse umeed afza (promising) lag rahi hai? Humme jaan na hoga ke hum us pechida kahani ka kon sa hissa dikhane walay hain. Class me aaj, mujhe das (10) aese thumbnails chahiye jo is ko explore (kholay/soch-wichar karay).*

Jab sabhi students ne apna critique (jaiza) khatam kar liya, unhone khamoshi se kaam karna shuru kiya aur das (10) naye sketches banaye. Baad mein, Pike ne mujhe bataya ki kuch students class ke dauran das thumbnails pure nahi kar paye, kyunki unhone har ek sketch par bohot zyada waqt barbad (spent too much time) kar diya tha. Wo iski ummeed hi (expecting) kar rahe the; yeh hamesha hota hai. Pike ke das thumbnails maangne ki ek wajah students ko tez (quickly) kaam karne par majboor (force) karna hai. Isse unhe kisi ek tasveer par zyada waqt lagane se bachaya jata hai. Unki buniyadi fitrat (instinct) yeh hoti hai ki wo shuru mein hi ek badiya (single great idea) idea par focus karein; assignment constraints is aadat ko khatam (block) karti hain aur iski jagah process par zyada dhyan (focus) mangti hain. Is time constraint (waqt ki bandish) ka ek aur fayda yeh hai ki students ko un cheezon par focus karna padta hai jo *sach mein (needs)* tasveer mein honi chahiye. Agar wo kisi tasveer par bohot waqt kharch kareinge, toh wo bohot zyada pechida (complicated) ban jayegi. Pike ke colleague Arny Nadler ne bhi ek aesi hi misaal (assignment) batayi, jo unhone apni sculpture (moti-saji) class me di thi: Students ko limits (quota) di jayein taki wo kisi ek par bhi bohot zyada dhyan na laga sakein. Unhone explain (wazahat) kiya:

*Pehle sculpture ki class me hamare paas samaan aur mavad (materials) ka ek bada dher hota tha. Aur tum sirf wohi bekaar cheezein (crap) istemal kar sakte the jo tum uss kamre (room) me paate the. Tumhe Monday ko ek piece bana kar dikhana hota tha, phir har aane wale din ek naya piece banate, aur Friday ko main un sab (5 pieces) ka review (muaayina) leta. Aisi pabandiyon (deadlines) me tumhe yeh sooliyat (luxury) nahi milti thi ki tum farigh beth kar khayalon (navel-gazing) me gum raho. Aakhir mein, agar paancho (5) bekaar the, toh theek hai. Lekin das (10) me se nau (9) dafa, uss safar (process) me koi cheez zaroor umeed ki kiran (spark) de jati.*

### **Naye (Creative) Ideas Ki Tadat (Range) Bahrana**

Ab aapki baari hai. Aglay das (10) second mein, paanch (5) kism ke jaanwaron (animals) ke naam lein. Main aapke liye "cat (billi)" se shuruat karta hoon.

*Paanch jaanwaron ke baare mein sochne ke liye thoda thehrein (Pause).*

Nahi, waqayi! Padhna jaari rakhne se pehle paanch jaanwaron ke baare mein sochein . . .

Kya aapne soch liya? Yahan mera andaza (prediction) hai: Aapki list mein sirf wo jaanwar hain jinke chaar (4) pair hain. Aapne shayad keedon (worms), machliyon (fish), ya machharon (mosquitos) ke baare mein nahi socha hoga. Kisi ek khas daira (narrow conceptual range) ke andar hi rehna, ye har insaan ki zahni aadat (cognitive instinct) hoti hai. Yeh baat tab aur zyada asar karti hai agar aapko pehle koi misaal (example) de di jaye; kyunki maine "cat" kaha, toh aapke dimaag mein chaar pairon wale doodh pilane wale jaanwar (mammals) aane ke liye taiyar (primed) ho gaye. Idea generation (Naye ideas paida karna) tab aur zyada creativity lata hai jab ideas ek doosre se bahut alag hon. Art aur design ke naye (beginning) students bhi wahi dimaagi aadat (cognitive habit) rakhte hain jo hum sab mein hoti hai: unke ideas sirf kuch aam (core) concepts ke ird-gird hi rehte hain. Aisa hone se rokne ke liye, assignments mein aesi rukawatein (constraints) rakhi jati hain jo students ko alag-alag (broader range) ideas paida karne mein madad karti hain. Tasavvur (Imagine) karein agar main aapse paanch jaanwaron ke baare mein sochne ko kehta, lekin sath mein yeh bhi kehta ki "koi bhi chaar pairon wala jaanwar nahi."

John Hendrix aksar apne illustration students ko kam se kam teen (3) sketches ya drafts laane ka kehte hain, aur unhe ek dusre se jitna mumkin ho sake alag (different) banane ke liye, unhe "bilkul ulat (polar opposite)" sochne ka mashwara (advises) dete hain:

*Hum wusat (range) par bohot zyada zor (stress) dete hain. Agar aap koi idea bana rahe hain aur aapko lagta hai ki yeh ek bahut hi mazboot (solid) idea hai, toh yeh ek aam baat (tendency) hoti hai ki aap ek aesa idea banayenge jo kafi milta julta ho, lekin thodi variation (fark) ke sath. Isliye hum ye kehte hain, "Okay. Us idea mein jo cheez kaam kar rahi hai use lein aur bilkul ulat (polar opposite) ki taraf jayein. Jaise abhi, yeh scale shift (size badalne) ki wajah se kaam kar raha hai, yani koi cheez badi hai aur kuch chhoti hai." Toh main poochta hoon, "Kya hoga agar tum use ulat (reverse) do? Ya kya hoga agar tum us visual metaphor (dikhnay wali misaal) ke liye kisi doosre object ko rakh (substitute) do?" Hum sach mein jitna ho sake utni wusat (range) haasil karne ki koshish karne par hosla (encourage) badhate hain. Unhe shuru mein yeh itna acha nahi aata, aur yeh ek aesi jagah hai jahan aap sach mein student ke kaam ko professional ke kaam se alag dekhte hain: unke ideas mein wusat (range) paida karne ki kabiliyat.*

Pike ki illustration class mein, maine unhe ek student ko yeh advice dete hue suna: "Khud ko is sasti susti (doldrums) se nikalne (snapping ourselves out) ka ek tareeqa yeh hai ki hum iske ulat (opposite) ka tasavvur (imagine) karein. Ek cheez ki jagah doosri cheez ko badalna (substituting) aksar kafi acha kaam karta hai."

**Professional artists aur designers ne ek apna khusoosi (recognizable personal) style bana liya hai, lekin iska yeh matlab nahi ki unhone apni wusat (range) ko mehdood (limiting) kar diya hai. Jab students kisi professional ka kaam dekhte hain aur unki aawaz (visual voice) ko pehchante hain, toh wo yeh sochte hain ki professionals ki creativity mein kami (narrow) aagayi hai. Lekin John Hendrix ka man-na hai ki tajurbekar professionals (experienced professionals) ke paas waqayi mein ek behtareen (greater) wusat (range) hoti hai: "Bhale hi unka point of view zyada theek (concise) ho, unki sochne ki wusat (range of thinking) aam taur par bahut zyada broad (chaudi) hoti hai. Soch mein badi wusat aam taur par wo cheez hoti hai jo lambe arse tak cultivate (tayar) ki jati hai."**

Students ko zyada options (possibilities) generate (paida) karne mein madad karne ke liye, kai professors in do steps wale assignment structure ka istemal karte hain:

* Class 1. Students se ek tay shuda ginti (specific number) ki ideas ya possibilities banayein, aur agali class mein sath lekar aane ko kahen.
* Class 2. Jab wo ideas lekar aayein, toh unse kehein ki har ek idea le kar uska bilkul ulat (polar opposite) karein.

Yeh aur zyada behtareen tareeqe se kaam karta hai jab students pehla step karte hue yeh na jante hon ki unhe baad mein uska ulat bhi karna hoga.

Beth Grabowski (UNC) is assignment structure ka istemal isliye karti hain taaki wo students ki "yeh jan-ne mein madad kar sakein ki unke apne dimaagi (biases) kya hain, aur unki apni pasand (aesthetic) kya hai. Meri kai classes mein, khas taur par shuruati classes mein, mera maqsad (what I do) yeh hota hai ki students ko unke khud ke mizaaj (aesthetic temperament) ya unki pasand (aesthetic preference) ko samajhne (understanding) mein madad karun, aur usay challenge karu ya isme izaafa (grow that change) karu." Wo kuch aam taur par paye jane wale polar opposites ko bayaan (describes) karti hain: "Ek khoobsurat (beautiful) cheez banane ki bajaye ek gandi (ugly) cheez banana, ya ek bohot hi choti (miniature) cheez banana aur phir ek bohot hi badi (huge) cheez banana, chahe wo bilkul ulat he kyun na ho. Ye bilkul ek mukhtasar raasta (shorthand) hai is baatcheet ka ki hum structure (dhancha) aur roop (form) mein kis tarah maani (meaning) ko daalte hain."

Kisi student ka ulat (opposite) kaam karna khusoosan us waqt behtar kaam aata hai jab wo kisi muqaam par atak gaye hon (stuck). Varland, SCAD ke ek architect, ne mujhe bataya, "Ek niyam (motto) hai jise hum mein se kai professors istemal karte hain: Agar aap phas (stuck) jate hain, toh kuch aur try karein. Jo aap kar rahe hain use chhod (stop) dein, aur kisi nayi taraf mudh (shift) jayein. Apna waqt zaya (waste) na karein. Main students se kehti hoon ki unhone jo thesi (khayal) bunayi hai, uska bilkul ulat (antithesis) karein aur use samajhne ki koshish karein. Yeh dhoondhne ke liye ki usme kaunsi aesi khamiyan (holes) hain jo unhe agay bharnay nahi de rahin."

Chapter 2, "Materials" mein, maine SCAD ke ek sculpture professor ke baare mein ek kahani sunai thi jise, apne career ke aaghaz (early in his career) mein, yeh laga tha ki usne apna ek anokha tareeqa (teaching technique) ijad (invent) kiya hai. Unhone saalo baad (years later) seekha ki kai dusre professors bhi aesi (similar) he practices apnate hain. Iska main idea yeh tha ki students kisi material ko uski asal halat (usual context) se hata den (displace). Yeh student ko uksati (jostles) hai kyunki yeh material ke maino (connotations) ko poori tarah tabdeel (changes) kar deti hai. "Yeh sikhana bohot aasan (very simple) hai. Misaal ke taur par, aap nails (keelon) se hammer (hathora) bana sakte hain." Ek dusri technique jo wo apne sculpture students ke sath istemal karte hain, wo yeh hai ki pehle wo unse ek *additive* sculpture banane ko kehte hain, jiska matlab hai, misaal ke taur par, ki aap clay (mitti) se shuru karte hain aur ise object ka aakar (shape) dete hain. Phir wo unse usi sculpture ko ek *subtractive* technique ka istemal karke banane ko kehte hain, jiska matlab hai ki aap stone (patthar) ke ek block se shuru karte hain aur aap isey tab tak tarashte (carve) hain jab tak ki aapka object nazar (appears) nahi aa jata. "Main aisa isliye karta hoon taaki unka dimaag badla (switch) ja sake. Yeh soch kar unhe mushkil hoti hai, magar ajeeb tor par bohot faydemand hai. Jab student isay model karne se le kar, tarashne par aate hain toh unka un cheezon ke hawale se, zehan puri tarah tabdeel ho jata hai. Unka is nateejay par pohnchna ke waqai aesa mumkin hai. Is cheez ne ek object ke, hawa mein tayrnay ke amal (form in space) ko bilkul double kardiya, sirf ek project se. Ye wakei waqai heran kun (amazing) hai."

### **Jab Aapko Constraints ki Zaroorat Nahi Rehti (When You No Longer Need Constraints)**

BFA program ke har saal ke sath, jaise-jaise students creative practice mein engage hona seekhte hain, wo seekhte hain ki dheema (slow down) kaise hona hai aur ek deliberate (soche-samjhe) process mein kaise engage hona hai. Unhe successful hone ke liye itni zyada constraints ki zaroorat nahi hoti. BFA program ke teesre aur chauthe saal mein, projects lagatar zyada open ended (khule) hote jate hain aur inme kaam ke lambe periods (waqt) aur kam constraints (rukawatein) hoti hain. Kabhi-kabhi ek hi project poora semester le leta hai.

Teesre saal tak, students kam constraints ke sath kaam karne ke liye taiyar (ready) hote hain. Amy Thompson (book arts, WashU) kehti hain ki "ek naya student (freshman) ya doosre saal ka student (sophomore) ko zyada constraints ki zaroorat ho sakti hai, jabki ek junior ya senior ko kam constraints ki zaroorat ho sakti hai, bas isliye ki ek project thoda aur openended ho sake. Bade (Older) students ise chhote (younger) students se thoda behtar dhang se handle (sambhal) kar sakte hain." Lisa Bulawsky, WashU mein ek artist, teesre aur chauthe saal ke students ko padhati hain. Jis tareeqe se wo apna course design karti hain, "unka 80 percent kaam selfdetermined (khud tay kiya hua) hota hai." Lekin in advanced students ko bhi kuch constraints se fayda (benefit) hota hai; semester ke shuru mein, wo unhe shuruat (started) karne ke liye ek-hafte ka project deti hain. "Bas isliye ki wo apni azaadi (freedom) ki waja se ghabra na jayein (paralyzed)."

Assignment constraints kisi bhi artist ya designer ki education mein ek aarzi (temporary) qadam hote hain. Pehle aur doosre saal ke students ko in supports ki bohot zyada zaroorat (need) hoti hai, lekin aakhiri maqsad (goal) students ko aesi manzil (place) par pahunchana hota hai jahan wo baghair kisi bahari maddad (support) ke apna khud ka creative process chala sakein. Cheryl Wassenaar, WashU ki ek painter aur sculptor, ne isey kuch is tarah bayaan (put it this way) kiya:

*Yahan unke 4 saal poore hone ke baad, wo khud ke bose hain (on their own). Aap asaan shabdon mein baag dhorr (reins) unke haath mein sonp dete hain. Ab wo khud he apne taya karda rules (parameters) ko structure (tartiib dena) dete hain, wo apne khud ke sawalaat puchte (questions) hain aur inko set karte (structure) hain, aur is silsilay (method) ko bhi tayaab aur set karte hain. Aur aap unhein naseehat detay hain.*

---

## **Chapter 7 - AMBIGUITY (Uljhan)**

**"Art banane ke dauran, kabhi-kabhi aap yeh bhi nahi jante ki aap kahan ja rahe hain. Aap bas is an-dekhe (invisible) goal ki taraf badh rahe hote hain. Aapke paas waqayi mein kho jane (being lost) ke kabil hone, ya yeh baat qubool karne ka mizaj (disposition) hona chahiye ki aapko agay kya ho ga iska ilm nahi hai. Bahut se students ke liye, isey apnana bahut mushkil hota hai—future ka na pata hona, yeh na pata hona ki wo kahan ja rahe hain, aakhiri nateeje (end result) ka na pata hona, aur is baat ka na pata hona ki unhe 'A' grade milega ya nahi."**

Cathy Rosamond, multimedia sculptor, School of Visual Arts

Dekhna seekhna (Learning to see) uljhan (ambiguity) wali jagah mein hota hai. Creativity kisi aisi jagah nahi ho sakti jahan sab kuch pehle se tay (certainty) ho. Bina uljhan ke, students ek asali (authentic) creative process ka tajurba (experience) nahi kar pate. Art aur design assignments isliye openended (khule) hote hain taaki har student ko problem ke baare mein sochne ka apna tareeqa dhoondhne aur process ke dauran apna rasta khud chun-ne ka mauka mil sake. Humne pichle chapter mein seekha tha ki ache assignments mein kai constraints (rukawatein) aur parameters (niyam) hote hain, lekin phir bhi wo creative exploration (khoj) ke liye jagah chhodte hain. Washington University in St. Louis (WashU) mein illustration padhane wale Jeff Pike ne mujhe bataya: "Aapko unhe uljhan (ambiguity) mein aage lekar (lead) jana hota hai. Un sabko sahi jawab dhoondhne ki training di gayi hai." Dekhna seekhne ke liye structure (dhanche) aur ambiguity (uljhan) ke beech ek dhyan se banaya gaya (careful) balance zaroori hai.

Problem yeh hai ki, Pike ne aage kaha, "students ise bilkul pasand nahi karte (just hate it)." Students ke paas aage badhne ka koi waazeh (obvious) rasta nahi hota. Unhe nahi pata hota ki aakhir tak pahunchne ke liye unhe kya karna hoga. Yeh niraash karne wala (frustrating) aur pareshani (anxiety) badhane wala hota hai. Lekin agar students ko ek aesa assignment diya jaye jisme koi uljhan (ambiguity) na ho, jisme exactly kya karna hai iske baare mein be-tahasha (extremely) detailed instructions hon, toh instructor (teacher) asal mein student ko assignment pura karne ka tareeqa bata raha hota hai. Ek student yeh soch kar mutmaeen (confident) ho sakta hai ki wo assignment sahi se kar raha hai, lekin uski creative kabiliyat (creative potential) khatam ho jati hai. Dekhna seekhne ke liye, aapko ek asali (authentic) creative process se guzarna hota hai, aur usme uljhan (ambiguity) shamil hoti hai. Ek professor ki dhyan se ki gayi guidance (rehnumai) mein is uljhan ke sath deal (negotiating) karke, aap dheere-dheere dekhna seekhte hain. Douglas Dowd, jo WashU mein illustration padhate hain, samjhate hain ki wo assignment par kaam shuru karne se pehle students ko detailed instructions kyun nahi dete:

*Main unke interests ki hifazat (protecting) kar raha hoon, yeh pakka (making sure) karke ki unhe aese tajurbe (experiences) milein jo un cheezon ko unke andar bitha (embed) dein jinhe samajhna unke liye faydemand (useful) hoga—us knowledge aur experience ko unke andar bithana jo unki apni (belongs to them) hai. Meri nahi. Main bahut saari explanation (wazahat) deta hoon, lekin wo hamesha aakhir mein hoti hai. Phir wo kehte hain, "Aapne mujhe yeh do hafte pehle kyun nahi bataya? Maine apna itna waqt barbad kar diya." Wo beech mein gussa (pissed) ho jate hain.*

Dowd ke students uljhan (ambiguity) par kaisa react (respond) karte hain? Wo uljhan ko kam karne aur certainty (tay-shuda baaton) ko badhane ki koshish karte hain. "Wo kahenge, 'Kya aap yahi chahte hain?' aur main kahunga, 'Khair, mujhe nahi pata. Yeh *mera* project nahi hai. Yeh *tumhara* hai.' Aur wo is baat se bahut pareshan (frustrated) ho jate hain." Toh maine unse poocha, kya hoga agar koi student aapki baat ko poori tarah maan le aur yeh faisla kare ki use waqayi un parameters (niyamon) se bahar jane ki zaroorat hai jo aapne project ke liye tay kiye hain? "Uss haalat (case) mein, main kahunga, 'Okay, main samajh gaya ki tumne aesa kyun kiya. Lekin yeh hamein wahan nahi le jayega jahan hum jana chahte hain. Isliye main isey ek taraf (aside) rakh raha hoon. Mujh par vishwas (Trust) karo ki mere isey ek taraf rakhne ki ek wajah hai. Tumhe abhi ke liye yahin (over here) rehne ki zaroorat hai.'" Maher (Skilled) professors uljhan (ambiguity) aur rukawat (constraint) ke beech dhyan se balance banane ke kabil hote hain. Dowd aage kehte hain:

*In students ko yeh game khelna aata hai. Unhone yeh game bahut pehle khelna shuru kar diya tha. Unhe iske liye inaam (rewarded) bhi mile hain. Toh unke paas ise jari rakhne (continue) ki har wajah hai. Yeh creativity ke raste mein aata hai. Yeh ek creative field mein ek asardaar (effective) professional banne ke raste mein aata hai. Sahi jawab kya hai, yeh pata lagane ki koshish karna. Uljhan se nafrat (The loathing of ambiguity). Ek creative mahoul (landscape) mein asardaar hone ka matlab hai ki aapko pata ho ki uljhan bhari duniya mein kaise kaam (operate) karna hai.*

Students ke ambiguity (uljhan) ke baare mein pareshan (anxious) hone ka ek bada karan unke grade (marks) ki fikar (concern) hai. Kabhi-kabhi students Dowd se poochte hain ki kisi project par A grade laane ke liye unhe kya karne ki zaroorat hai. Wo kahenge, "Kya tum mujhse pooch rahe ho ki A grade kaise layen? Main tumhe yeh nahi bata sakta." Wo aage kehte hain: "Hum is ambiguity wali baat ko mazboot (reinforce) karne ke liye shuru mein hi unhe bata dete hain, 'Okay. Tumhe grades nahi milenge. Tumhe aakhir mein ek grade milega, lekin tumhe kisi bhi project par grade nahi milega.'" Students ko semester ke aakhir tak nahi pata hota ki unhe kya grade milne wala hai—aap tasavvur (imagine) kar sakte hain ki yeh kisi student ko kitna pareshan (anxious) kar sakta hai. Lekin kai professors ka man-na hai ki aakhir-kaar iska nateeja behtar creative learning mein nikalta hai.

Pichle chapter mein, humne dekha tha ki ache assignments mein sakht (severe) constraints hoti hain. Lekin bilkul sahi constraints design karna asaan nahi hai; assignment design karne ki ek art (kala) hoti hai. Naye (Beginning) professors aksar apne careers ki shuruat aese assignments se karte hain jo kafi structured (tarteeb-war) nahi hote. Iske ulat (Inversely), kabhi-kabhi kisi assignment mein bahut zyada parameters (niyam) hote hain aur uljhan (ambiguity) bahut kam hoti hai. Lekin aap yeh tab tak nahi jaan sakte jab tak ki project poora na ho jaye aur aap yeh na dekhein ki sabhi students ne aakhir tak ek kafi hadd tak seedha rasta (fairly linear path) apnaya hai. Yeh kafi kam (rare) hota hai; zyada-tar aesa hota hai ki naye professors bahut kam constraints ke sath shuruat karte hain. Yahan tak ki tajurbekar (experienced) professors bhi, jab wo ek naya assignment banate hain, toh aam taur par agli baar jab wo course padhate hain toh usme aur constraints jod dete hain.

Ambiguity (uljhan) ke bina, students problem ko samajhne aur solve karne ka apna rasta dhoondhne ke process se nahi guzarte. Jab students professors se aur zyada specific details mangte hain, toh zyada-tar Dowd ki tarah hi hote hain—wo inkar (demur) kar dete hain. SCAD mein padhane wali architect Julie Varland ne mujhe bataya:

*Kayi baar students poochte hain, "Khair, aap kya dekhna chahte hain?" [Hasti hain.] Saalo (years) mein yeh lagatar (increasingly) badhta ja raha hai. Yahan tak ki chauthe (fourth) saal ke students aur graduate students ke sath bhi, jo waqayi mein pareshan (dismaying) karne wala hai. Aisa lagta hai jaise unhe sirf grade mein dilchaspi (interested) hai, sach mein seekhne ke bajaye professor ko khush karne (pleasing) mein. Wo khud isey dhoondhne (figuring it out) ki kadi mehnat (hard work) nahi karna chahte.*

Maine poocha, "Aap unhe is tarah sochne se kaise bahar nikalti hain (push them out)?" Unhone jawab diya:

*Main wahi sawal un par wapas daal deti hoon (turn the question around). Main course ke objectives (maqsad) par wapas jati hoon aur unse poochti hoon, kya aapko lagta hai ki professor ke paas pehle se tay kiye gaye (pre-prescribed) processes, ya ideas hain jinke liye main bas intezar kar rahi hoon ki aap mujhe deliver (de) karein? Aur aam taur par, mujhe yeh sawal dobara sun-ne ko nahi milta. Aap jante hain, main unhe sharminda (embarrass) nahi karna chahti, lekin main chahti hoon ki wo samjhein ki agar aap ek serious (sanjida) learner hain toh yeh be-tahasha bewaqoofi bhara (incredibly silly) sawal hai. Kabhi-kabhi yeh unke liye bahut niraash (frustrating) karne wala hota hai. Lekin mujhe lagta hai ki iske aakhir mein jo learning curve (seekhne ka nateeja) aata hai, khaas taur par in saalon (years) mein, mujhe lagta hai ki jin students se main baad mein baat karti hoon, unhe us mauke (opportunity) se kahin zyada faida (got so much more out of) mila hota hai, uski tulna mein (compared to) agar main unke level par utar aati (pandering to their level).*

Rubin Salinas ki SCAD mein illustration class mein, maine ek student ke car designs ke thumbnail sketches ka critique dekha. Student ne isey do sketches tak mehdood (narrowed it down) kar liya hai lekin yeh tay nahi kar pa raha ki project ke baaki hisse ke liye kisey aage badhaye (pursue). Wo chahta hai ki Salinas use batayen ki kaun sa sabse best hai: "Kya aapki koi pasand (preference) hai? Mujhe kaun sa chun-na chahiye?" Yeh ek an-kaha (unstated) lekin samjha ja sakne wala (implied) sawal hai, ki kis par sabse acha grade milne ki sambhavna hai? Salinas is sawal ko taal dete (deflects) hain: "Yeh pasand (preference) ke baare mein nahi hai. Yeh is baare mein hai ki aapko kya lagta hai kaam karne wala hai (going to work)." Phir Salinas open (khule) sawalon ki ek series poochte hain, jiska goal student ko yeh seekhne mein madad karna hai ki creative decisions lene ke baare mein kaise socha jaye: "Mujhe in views ke baare mein batao jo yahan hain [ek drawing ki taraf ishara karte hue]. Kya tumne is baare mein socha ki yeh kin umaron (ages) ke liye hai?" Wo aakhir mein (concludes) yeh kehte hue baat khatam karte hain: "Aapke liye sabse zyada dhyan kheenchnay wala (eye-catching) kya hoga? Sabse zyada dilchasp (interesting)? Shayad main khud se yahi poochta."

Jab maine yeh mulaqat (encounter) dekhi, toh mujhe koi idea nahi tha ki Salinas ko dono sketches mein se kaun sa zyada pasand tha, aur mujhe yakeen hai ki student ko bhi nahi pata tha. Yeh jaanbujh kar (intentional) tha. Professors chahte hain ki students apne khud ke creative decisions lene ki kabiliyat mein maharat (master) haasil karein.

Yahan tak ki sabse zyada focused assignments bhi usse kahin zyada ambiguous (uljhe hue) hote hain jiske aadi (used to) students high school aur dusre academic subjects mein hote hain. Jab unhe yeh open-ended assignments diye jate hain, toh students aksar professor se yeh batane ko kehte hain ki unhe aage kaise badhna (proceed) chahiye, ya wo poochte hain ki kya wo assignment sahi dhang se (correctly) kar rahe hain. Joe DiGioia, SCAD ke graphic design professor ki ek studio class mein, ek student ne apni beecho-beech ki (interim) presentation ka nateeja (summarized) nikalte hue kaha: "Toh, ab main poori tarah se confuse (uljhan mein) hoon ki kis disha (direction) mein jau [afsos se (ruefully) hasti hai]." Salinas ke student ki tarah, yeh student bhi professor ke intezar mein hai ki wo use batayen ki aage kya karna hai. Lekin, Salinas ki tarah hi,

DiGioia ne usay yeh nahi bataya ki kya karna hai; uski bajaye unhone kuch open-ended sawalaat (aisay sawal jinka jawab lamba ho) pooche, jisme yeh bhi shamil the:

*Is ke sath hamara pehla tajurba (experience) kya hoga? Humay yahan aur kis cheez ki zaroorat (need) hai?*

*Aap kya sochte hain?*

Ek sau (100) se zyadah lines ke dialouge ke baad, DiGioia ne review is baat pe khatam kiya:

*Sochein ke woh (ideas) kaisay dikhege. Jis bhi taraf aap jana chahay.*

Phir wo aglay student ki janib review (critique) ke liye barh gaye.

Aap student ko yeh nahi bata saktay ke konsa rasta (path) chunna hai kyunke yeh unse (students se) apni creative choices lene ka mauqa (opportunity) chheen leta hai. In choices ko lene ke liye sochnay ke naye tariqay (new way of thinking) seekhnay ki zaroorat hai.

Ek behtareen creative process ba-dastoor (iterative) aur sidha nahi (nonlinear) hota. Creativity tab jhalakti (thrives) hai jab azaadi ya ghair yaqeeni (ambiguity) ho. Agar sab kuch tay-shuda (certain) ho, toh aakhri maqsad (endpoint) tak ka rasta bohot wazeh aur seedha (obvious and straight) hota hai. Jab bahut kuch ghair-yaqeeni (uncertain) ho, toh rasta saaf nahi hota; wahan nayay raastay talash (trailblazing) karne ki zaroorat hoti hai. Agar koi ambiguity (ghair-yaqeeni surat-e-haal) nahi hai, toh creativity ki bhi zaroorat nahi hai. Aise halaat mein jahan sab kuch tay shuda ho (complete certainty), wahan ahmiyat (priorities) kaam ki tezi (efficiency), raftaar (speed), aur mukammal honay (perfection) ko di jati hai. Yeh tareeqa manufacture plant, health care, food safety aur bohot se aam school subjects mein hota hai. Hum in jagho pe ghair yeqeeni (uncertainty) nahi chahte; humein durustagi (accuracy) aur sachai (correctness) chahiye hoti hai. Lekin creativity ghair yaqeeni (ambiguity) me panapti (flourishes) hai. Tajurbaat (Experimentation) aur naye raston ki talash (exploration) rooz ka kaam (daily practice) hai. Wahan na-kamiya (failures) aur band raste (dead ends) bhi ayenge. Safar mein thehrao ayega, rukh badlega aur phir se shuru hoga.

Ambiguity aesi cheezon (unexpected) ko namoodaar (emergence) hone mein madad deti hai jin ki umeed na ho.

### **The Princess of Reassurance (Tasalli Ki Shehzadi)**

WashU ke mujasama saaz (sculptor), Ron Leax ne mujhe apne ek student ke baray mein bataya:

*Hum use "Princess of Reassurance" kehte hain. Aap jante hain, har pandra (15) minute baad wo ek sawal poochti hai: "Kya yeh theek hai?" Ya "Yahan aaiye, dekhiye maine kya kiya." Aur main kamre ke dusre hisse mein hota hoon, aur main bas kehta hoon, "Oh, tumhein bas tasalli (reassure) chahiye. Main wahan nahi aa raha." Aur wo kehti hai, "Haan, aap theek keh rahe hain." Aur wo wapas kaam par lag jati hai. Aaj, uske sath ek aur jawan ladki kaam kar rahi thi jo bas aage badhti ja rahi thi, aur maine kaha, "Theek hai, tum Queen of Tenacity (himmat ki malka) ho. Aur ab se tumhe iske sawalon ka jawab dena hoga. Mujhe nahi."*

Leax ek chahay janay walay (beloved) ustad hain jinhe WashU mein sabse behtareen (highest) course evaluations (raye) milti hain. "Students mere paas aayenge aur har waqt mujhse poochenge, kahenge, 'Kya main yeh kar sakta hoon?' aur main kehta hoon, 'Mujhse yeh mat poocho. Agar tum mujhse aesa karne ko kehte ho, toh tumne mujhe apne kaam (piece) ka malik (owner) bana diya hai. Meri class ka wahid asool (rule) yeh hai ki jab tak main khud na kahun, tum aesa 'nahi' kar sakte.'" Semester ke dauran, wo har hafte badalte halaaton (fluid ways) ke hisab se improvisation (mauqe par badlaav) karte hain jo ambiguity (uljhan) aur constraint (rukawat) ke balance ko theek (adjust) karte hain. Unke colleague Jeff Pike ne kaha, "Wo ambiguity (uljhan) ke issue (masle) se sidhi takkar (frontal assault) lete hain aur ek tarah se unhe pool ke gehre hisse mein fek dete hain. Aur phir unhe guide (rehnumai) karte hain." Maine jin professors ka interview liya, unme se kuch ke paas kai pages ke be-tahasha (extremely) detailed syllabi the. Leax ne mujhe apna ek syllabus dikhaya: yeh sirf do pages ka tha, aur isme yeh do paragraphs shamil the:

Special Topics ke liye Syllabus: Studio Methods: Artists Studio mein Faisle (Decisions) Kaise Lete Hain, Fall 2010, Art

*Un macro aur micro taqato (forces) ki tehqeeqat (investigation) jo form (shakal) ke banne ke buniyaadi asoolon (generative principles) ko asar-andaaz (influence) karti hain. Autonomous (khud-mukhtar) agents, moment (pal), analogous thought (milte-julte khayalat), spontaneous organization (apne aap ban-na), autocatalytic sets, phase transition states, QWERTY Theory, work cycles (kaam ke daur), inference (andaza) aur deduction (nateeja), response (jawab) aur assertion (daave) ke concepts (tasavvuraat) ko examine (parkha) kiya jayega ki wo insaan ke kaam karne ke process par kaise asar dalte hain. Course ko chaar hisson mein baanta jayega: matrix thinking, formal thinking, analogous thinking aur autocatalytic thinking.*

*Yeh ek open-ended aur self-determining (khud tay karne wali) class hai jiska main defining product (sabse eham nateeja) student ka kaam hoga. Students apne sathiyon (peers) aur instructor (ustad) ke sath milkar creative work project, assessment (janchne) ka tareeqa aur kamyabi ka criteria (me'yar) tay karenge. Maqsad ki sanjeedgi (Seriousness of purpose), tehqeeq (investigation) ka daira aur samjh mein aane wale (coherent) nateeje be-had zaroori (essential) honge. Poore semester aur aakhiri presentation par kaam ki wazahat (Articulation) aur uska difaa (defense) be-had eham (critical) hoga.*

Pike bilkul theek keh rahe the! Yeh be-tahasha (extremely) abstract (khayali) aur conceptual hai. Lekin jab main studio mein Leax ke piche-piche gaya aur unhe apne students ke sath interact (baatcheet) karte hue suna, toh maine "autocatalytic sets" ya "matrix thinking" jaise alfaaz nahi sune. Leax ki classes mein, maine technical details ke baare mein bahut si kaam aane wali (practical) advice dekhi. Yahan ek aisi advice hai jo unhone ek student ko di thi jo ek nimbu (lemon) ki murti (sculpture) bana rahi thi, jise paanch guna bada (scaled up five times) kiya gaya tha (is assignment ke baare mein introduction mein bayan (described) kiya gaya hai, aur figure 0.6 mein is encounter (mulaqaat) ki ek photo hai):

*Yeh grayer green (halka hara) hai, aur yeh thoda aur burnt umber (gehra bhura) hai, ek sach mein high-value burnt umber. Agar aap bade brushes ka istemal karte hain, toh aap waqayi (really), ek tarah ka mottled application (dhabbe-dar tareeqa) karna chahte hain. Aap brush strokes nahi chhodna chahte. Us brush ka istemal na karein, yeh thoda zyada nokeela (pointy) hai. Apne object ko ek magnifying glass ke zariye dekhein taaki aapko samajh aaye ki us surface (satah) ka look kaisa hai. Aap shayad isey ek bahut bade naram brush se lagana chahein, ya shayad ek sponge ke sath. Wapas peeche jayein, kam se kam das (10) foot peeche jayein aur ise us tarah se dekhein.*

Colleagues (Sathi) is baat se sehmat hain ki Leax ki classes sabse zyada open ended (khuli/bina pabandiyon wali) hoti hain; wo apne ambiguous (uljhan wale) assignments ke liye mash-hoor (legendary) hain. Lekin phir bhi, materials behad zaroori (essential) hain. Banana hi sochna hai (Making is thinking). Craft (hunar) ke bina koi abstraction (khayali kaam) nahi hota.

### **"Mujhe Unhe Chedna (Poke) Pasand Hai": Zyada Aaramdeh (Comfortable) Na Hon**

**Aap waqt ke sath seekhte hain ki aapko khud ko us point (muqaam) tak pahunchane ke liye manana (convince) hoga jahan aap nahi jante ki aap kya kar rahe hain. Theek us cheez ke kinare par jisme aap dilchaspi (interested) rakhte hain, bas usse thoda aage (beyond) jo aap kar sakte hain. Kyunki wahin par aap waqayi (actually) creative hote hain. Aur wo ek bahut hi bhayanak (awful) jagah hoti hai. Yeh bahut stress (tanaav) wali hoti hai aur yeh aapki saari insecutries (khauf/kamzoriyon) ko bahar le aati hai. Yeh mushkil hai. [Hasti hain.] Har baar yeh mushkil hota hai! Isliye har koi isse bachta (avoids) hai. Ek artist ke taur par, aapko ise khud karna seekhna hoga, lekin art students ke ek teacher ke taur par, aapko unhe iske sath aaramdeh (comfortable) banana hoga. Us jagah ko pehchan-ne (identify) ke liye. Koi wahan rehna nahi chahta.**

Lynn Tomaszewski, visual artist, School of the Art Institute of Chicago

Maine School of the Art Institute of Chicago (SAIC) campus mein Lynn Tomaszewski ka interview liya, jo Art Institute of Chicago ke theek bagal (right next) mein hai, jo duniya ke sabse behtareen (premier) art museums mein se ek hai. Unhone mujhe bataya: "Students ambiguity (uljhan) se pareshan (frustrated) ho jate hain. Khas taur par aalsi (lazy) students." Ambiguity (Uljhan) technique ke mukable conceptual (khayali) thinking ke sath zyada bada challenge hai:

*Students apni making (banane) ke mamle mein bahut disciplined (paband) hote hain, lekin apni thinking (soch) ke mamle mein kam. Unhone craft (hunar) par kuch maharat (mastery) hasil kar li hai aur unhe tareef (praise) bhi mili hai. Misaal ke taur par, wo ek behtareen drawer (banane wale) hain. Aur phir wo yahan aate hain aur ek faculty member unhe batata hai, "Khair, yeh toh bahut badiya hai, lekin iska duniya mein hone wali kisi cheez se kya talluq (relate) hai?" ya wo yeh bhi pooch sakte hain, "Aap un skills (hunar) mein se kisi ka bhi istemal kiye bina isey kaise karenge?"*

Dono hi halaton mein, student ek aasan rasta (easy path) chunta hai jo unhe ambiguity (uljhan) se bachne (evade) ki ijazat deta hai. Ek mahir (skilled) professor isey dekhega aur unhe wapas us jagah par laane (steering) ke zariye jawab dega jahan unhe ambiguity ka samna karna hoga.

Rhonda Arntsen Savannah College of Art and Design (SCAD) mein graphic design padhati hain. "Main umeed karti hoon ki wo bilkul shuruat (very beginning) se hi kuch aisa kar rahe hain jo bahut asahaj (uncomfortable) hai, aur hamare paas kamyaabi (successful) se kam ideas, kamyaabi se kam outcomes (nateeje) pane ke mauke (opportunities) hain." Agar wo shuruat mein comfortable hain, toh iska shayad yahi matlab hai ki wo ek aise tareeqe se soch rahe hain jo bahut linear (seedha/aasan) hai. Wo kafi risk nahi le rahe hain. Students ko process ki shuruat mein discomfort (asahajta) ke sath comfortable (sahaj) hona seekhna chahiye. Kuch saalo baad, wo aakhir-kaar process par vishwas (trust) karna seekh jate hain. Wo seekh jate hain ki ambiguity (uljhan) ki jagah wahi hai jahan possibilities (sambhavnayein) ubhar kar aati (emerge) hain.

Jab students bahut zyada comfortable hote hain, toh unhe wo uncertainty (ghair-yaqeeni surat-e-haal) ka tajurba (experience) nahi hota jo creativity ko ubharne (emerge) ke liye zaroori hai. Lekin hum creative discomfort (creativity wali be-chaini) ki baat kar rahe hain, emotional ya interpersonal (aapsi rishton ki) discomfort ki nahi. Aakhir-kaar, students ko yeh mehsoos karne ki zaroorat hai ki wo exploration (khoj) ke liye ek safe space (mehfooz jagah) mein hain, kyunki unhe apne professor aur dusre students ke samne fail hone ki bahut real (asli) possibility ka samna karna padta hai. Ek photographer jo kabhi SCAD ke teaching center ki director thi, ne mujhe bataya: "Aapke paas ek physical space (jagah) honi chahiye jo comfortable ho: sahi equipment (saman), proper lighting, controlled temperature. Professors ko apne students ke sath milkar kaam karna (collaborate) chahiye aur ek aesa mahoul (environment) banana chahiye jahan wo apne ideas ko bayan (express) karne ke liye azaad (free) hon, yeh jante hue ki unka critique (jaiza) liya jayega, lekin yeh koi niji hamla (personal attack) nahi hoga. Yeh har kisi ke liye ek aaramdeh (comfortable) mahoul (climate) hona chahiye." Lekin phir, wo aage wazahat (clarify) karti hain—yeh safe space (mehfooz jagah) creative discomfort ke ek zyada gehre (profound) roop ko mumkin banati (enables) hai, jo students ko aur zyada saaf taur par (clearly) dekhne ki taraf le jati hai. "Assignments ko unke comfortable zone se bilkul (just) aage hona chahiye, lekin ise unhe is par thoda sa push karna (dhakelna) hoga taaki unhe challenge (chunauti) mile aur wo motivate (prerit) hon."

SCAD mein, foundations padhane wale kuch professors ne ek informal (gair-rasmi) reading group banaya tha. Hum teaching ke baare mein dopahar ke khane (lunchtime) par conversation (baat-cheet) ke liye baithe. Hamari conversation baar-baar ambiguity (uljhan) par aakar ruk jati thi. Professors is baare mein baat kar rahe the ki kaise first-year students sabhi wahi karna chahte hain jo unhe pehle se karna aata hai. Linda ne apni teaching se yeh qissa (anecdote) sunaya:

*Creativity ka matlab perceptual rut (soch ke paimano) mein na phasna hai. Iski ek asaan misaal drawing mein milti hai. Main students se kahoongi ki sir (head) ke liye oval ya circle (gole) ka istemal na karein. Asal mein sir ko dekhein aur samjhein ki wahan haqeeqat (really) mein kya hai. Aap inki yeh shuruati (early) aadat dekhte hain ki wo har cheez ko ek pattern ya symbol ki tarah (symbolic manner) rakhna chahte hain. Wo wahi karte hain aur wo nahi chahte ki aap unhe koi alag tareeqa dikhayein kyunki wo isey poori tarah se (all the way) apne confidence (aatma vishwas) ko support karne ke liye apnana chahte hain.*

Baaki log haan mein sir hilate hain. Ek ne jawab diya, *Sahi kaha, kyunki wo unke liye asahaj (uncomfortable) hota hai.*

Linda aage boli: *Wo yeh sab apne andar daba ke (keep it all inside) rakhte hain . . .*

Ek teesre professor ne unki baat (sentence) poori ki: *. . . bajaye iske ki wo us cheez se seekhein (pull from) jo wo dekhte hain.*

Ek chauthe professor ne kaha: *Wo bas yeh janna chahte hain ki abhi (right now) us project par A grade kaise layen. Abhi unka bas yahi chhota sa daira (window) hai, abhi wo chhota sa project. Aur main acha (well) kaise karu.*

Linda ne sehmati jatai (agreed): *Unhe sirf qadam-ba-qadam (step by step) (hidayat) chahiye.*

Us waqt, SCAD mein poori university ke liye yeh policy (niyam) thi ki har course mein semester ke dauran kam se kam (minimum) paanch graded assignments hone chahiye. Reading group ko is policy par kadi aitraz (critical) tha. Yeh is baat ko mushkil banata hai ki "unka dhyan chamakte huye grades se hatakar process ke unke ownership (apnapan) par lagaya jaye. Haal hi mein yahan Ken Bain aaye the, aur unhone is baare mein bahut tameez (polite) se baat ki thi, lekin hum sab samajh gaye the (read between the lines), ki unka man-na tha ki jo system humein follow karna padta hai jisme kam se kam paanch grades ki zaroorat hoti hai, wo darasal is goal (maqsad) ke raste mein rukawat (stands in the way) banta hai." (Bain *What the best college teachers do* ke author/lekhak hain.) Baaki log haan mein sir hilate hain aur kehte hain, "haan (yeah)"; wo sab sehmat (agree) hain ki grades par students ka focus creativity ko rokta (blocks) hai.

Dusre kinare (extreme) par School of the Art Institute of Chicago hai, jo courses mein grades na dene ki apni policy ke liye mashhoor hai. Lekin iske alawa sab kuch professor ki marzi (discretion) par chhod diya jata hai: semester mein kitne projects dene hain; rubric (grading ka paimana) istemal karna hai ya nahi. (Lagbhag koi rubric istemal nahi karta, aur bahut se log openly is practice ke khilaf/critical hain.) Douglas Dowd (WashU) ne apne students ko bataya ki unhe specific assignments ke liye koi grades nahi milenge; wo unhe sirf aakhir mein ek final course grade dete hain.

Maine SAIC mein Tiffany Holmes ka interview liya (wo ab Ringling College of Art and Design mein academic affairs ki vice president ban gayi hain); unhone mujhe bataya:

*Hamari class mein hum ambiguity (uljhan) ke sath comfortable (sahaj) hone ke baare mein bahut baat karte hain, aur yeh ek aisi skill hai jo waqayi hona (worthwhile) zaroori hai. Hum real-world professional contexts (asal duniya ke peshawar halaaton) ke baare mein bahut baat karte hain, jahan ambiguity hoti hai, aur yeh assignment unhe iske liye kaise taiyar kar raha hai.*

Wo mujhe ek class ka example (misaal) deti hain jo wo art aur technology mein padhati hain, ki jab shuruaati (beginning) students ko ambiguity (uljhan) di jati hai toh wo kya karte hain. Is assignment mein, students ko ek computer animation banane ke liye ek program likhna hota hai:

*Jis drawing class ke baare mein main aapko bata rahi thi, usme mera sabse pasandeeda pehla assignment jo main pehle chaar se chhah hafton ke andar deti hoon, wo yeh hai ki unhe waqt (time) ka ek animation banana hai, ek visual (dekhne wala) animation—jiska matlab hours, minutes, aur seconds hai. Toh yeh bas yeh pakka karne ka ek tareeqa hai ki wo samajhte hain ki code mein function kaise call (istemal) karna hai. Buniyaadi taur par, yeh ek visual watch (dekhne wali ghadi) hai. Jab unhe assignment milta hai, tab tak hum bahut saare preparatory (taiyari wale) problem sets kar chuke hote hain. Unhone pehle hi ek cheez (task) poori kar li hoti hai jisme unke paas ek minute ki line aur ek minute ka dot (bindu) hota hai. Unhe yeh dikhane ke liye bahut saare code examples (misalein) diye gaye hote hain ki kuch khaas cheezein kaise karni hain. Lekin jab main unhe yeh assignment deti hoon, toh wo lazman (inevitably) puchte hain, "Kya aap mujhe ek example dikha sakti hain?" [Hasti hain.] Unhe yeh na dikhana pade ki dusre students ne kya kiya hai, maine kuch funky, sasti (inexpensive), high-design watches (ghadiyon) ki tasveerein (snapshots) li hain jo waqt ka graphic version (roop) dikhati hain. Commercial (Bazar mein milne wale) products. Yeh unhe thoda aaram (at ease) deta hai, jab wo asal duniya (real world) ki koi aesi cheez dekhte hain jiska sach mein assignment se kuch lena-dena hai. Maine ek cheez sach mein seekhi hai ki aise assignments hona kitna zaroori hai jo students ko real-world objects aur experiences se jodte hain.*

"Ek classic (aam) sawal hota hai, 'Kya mujhe isey 2D banana hai?' Jaise, kya yeh ek fountain (fawwara) ho sakta hai, aapko pata hai." Wo kehti hain nahi, kam se kam shuru mein: "Mera jawab hota hai, 'Assignment ka maqsad (purpose) yeh pakka karna hai ki aap is cheez ko samajh gaye hain. Toh, kyun na aap yeh assignment karein, aur phir agar aap isey kisi aur cheez mein bada (expand) karna chahte hain, toh jaise-jaise aap class mein aage badhte hain, aap kar sakte hain.'" Dowd ki tarah, tajurbekaar (experienced) teachers jante hain ki student ki creativity badhane ke liye ambiguity ko kaise limited (mehdood) rakhna hai.

Lisa Bulawsky, WashU mein ek painter, apne students ko lagatar is baat par dhakka deti (pushing) rehti hain ki wo gaur (examine) karein ki wo kya kar rahe hain:

*Yeh ek lagatar chalne wala (constant sort of), "Khair, tum iske sath aur kya kar sakte ho? Tum us idea ko, ya image banane ke us tareeqe ko kaise challenge kar sakte ho?" ya jo kuch bhi yeh hai. Aap unhe grow (aage badhne) karne mein madad karne ki koshish kar rahe hote hain. Ek hi jagah ruk jane (Stagnation) se bacha jata hai. Har waqt (All the time) dhakka dena (Pushing). Misaal ke taur par, agar koi lagatar is scale par kaam kar raha hai, toh unhe kuch aesa try karne ke liye push karein jo unhe asahaj (uncomfortable) kar de. Hum students ki baat kar rahe hain, kisi established (mashhoor) artist ke career ki nahi. Unke paas apni baqi zindagi (rest of their lives) apne khud ke studio mein ek artist ke taur par . . . complacent (khud se mutmaeen) hone ke liye hai. [Hasti hain.] Unka focus bahut jaldi bahut narrow (tang/chota) ho jana unke liye khatarnak (dangerous) hai.*

Jab students ko pehli baar art aur design school ke ambiguous assignments milte hain, toh yeh azaadi (freedom) unhe uncomfortable kar deti hai. Wo school ko us nazariye (approach) se dekhne ke aadi (used to) hote hain jahan unka kaam professors ki expectations (umeedon) ko poora karna hota hai. Stephen Hall, jo SCAD mein advertising padhate hain, unhone mujhe bataya:

*Yeh unke nichle darje (lower-level) ki education mein saalon ki conditioning (aadat dalne) se aata hai, jahan unhe ek tarah se (so to speak) lino ke andar color karne, rules (niyamon) ko follow karne, aur expectations (umeedon) par khara utarne (meet) ki training di gayi hai. Agar expectations bahut saaf (clearly) batai gayi hon, toh wo un expectations ko poora karne ke liye kadi mehnat (work very hard) karenge. Jab main students ko us pattern se bahar aane ko kehta hoon, aur maine unhe bahut zyada azaadi di hoti hai, toh maine seekha hai ki students ko relax (loosen up) karne ke liye, mujhe unke behavior ko manzoor karne (approving) aur inaam dene (rewarding) ke baare mein bahut specific hona hoga, aur un solah (16) saal ki training ko undo (khatam) karne ki koshish karni hogi jo unhone sahi (doing it right) karne mein guzari hai. Main unhe khulne (open up) aur zyada creative banne ke liye encourage (prerit) kar raha hoon. Aur aesa karne ke liye, mujhe us conditioning (aadat) ko todna (break down) hoga jo unhe pehle se mil chuki hai.*

Hyesu Lee, jo School of Visual Arts (SVA) mein illustration padhati hain, unhone mujhe bataya ki unke students ambiguity par kaisa respond karte hain:

*Kuch students ko yeh pasand nahi aata. Wo bas wahi draw karna chahte hain jo draw karne mein wo ache (good at) hain. Wo ek hi cheez, ek hi concept, ek hi character par atak jate (fixated) hain. Wo explore nahi karna chahte kyunki yeh uncomfortable (asahaj) hota hai. Jab aap koi poori tarah se nayi cheez (totally new) draw karte hain, toh zahir hai ki pehli koshish mein koi bhi waqayi acha (really good) nahi hoga. Isliye wo kuch alag try karne se darrte (scared) hain. Isliye mujhe andar jaana aur unhe uncomfortable (asahaj) karna pasand hai. Mujhe unhe chhedna (poke) pasand hai. Agar wo sirf wahi karte hain jisme wo comfortable hain, toh phir class ka matlab hi kya hai? Kyunki yeh ek aisi safe jagah hai jahan aap mistakes (galtiyan) kar sakte hain aur hum iske baare mein baat kar sakte hain. Agar aap isse khush (happy) nahi hain, toh aap hamesha us par wapas ja sakte hain jisme aap comfortable hain.*

Carol Fabricatore (SVA) discomfort (asahajta) aur safety (hifazat), ambiguity aur risk ke beech balance banane ki zaroorat par zor (emphasizes) deti hain:

*Main chahti hoon ki mera studio ek aesa safe zone ho jahan unhe lage ki wo risks le sakte hain, khud ko aage la sakte hain (put themselves out there), nayi cheezein try kar sakte hain, aur dekh sakte hain ki kya hota hai. Main is baat par zor (stress) deti hoon ki aapko hamesha wo pasand nahi aayega jo nikal kar aayega (comes out). Jab log bas apne hisab se (intuitively) draw karte hain aur cheezein nikal kar aati hain, wo sach mein iske baare mein soch nahi rahe hote; kabhi-kabhi unhe yeh pasand nahi aata. Yeh utna polished (chamkdar) nahi hota jaisa wo tab kar sakte hain jab wo apne comfort zone mein hote hain. Main yeh kehne ke liye wahan hoon ki, yeh acha hai, isey dekho, tumhe ise thoda aur bahar nikalne (let this out a bit more) ki zaroorat hai.*

### **Yeh Nirash Karne Wala Hai Aur Yeh Theek Hai (It's Frustrating and That's Okay)**

Professional creatives ambiguity (uljhan) ka samna karte hue bhi phalte-phoolte (thrive) hain. Lekin ambiguity ko manage (sambhalne) karne ke liye, aapko yeh janna hoga ki dekhna kaise hai, warna aap kho jayenge (lost). Kyunki students abhi bhi dekhna seekh rahe hain, unhe tajurbekaar (experienced) artists aur designers ke mukable zyada constraints ki zaroorat hoti hai. Aur in constraints ke bawajood bhi, assignments mein usse kahin zyada ambiguity hoti hai jiske sath students aaramdeh (comfortable) mehsoos karte hain. Jaise-jaise students in assignments par kaam karte hain, unka frustrate (nirash) aur anxious (pareshan) hona bahut aam baat (common) hai. Professors is frustration ki umeed rakhte hain aur samajhte hain ki jab students is se guzar rahe hote hain toh yeh unke liye mushkil ho sakta hai. Lekin wo jante hain ki yeh frustration learning ke liye zaroori (necessary) hai, aur wo jante hain ki students ko baad mein ehsaas hoga ki ise aesa hi kyun hona tha. Lekin is waqt, students abhi tak yeh nahi jante ki dekhna kaise hai, isliye wo nahi samajhte ki constraints aur ambiguity is tarah se kyun design kiye gaye hain. SCAD ke ek first-year foundations professor ne mujhe bataya ki students "structure ki kami ko lekar niraash (frustrated)" ho jate hain, lekin course ke aakhir tak, "wo waqayi khush hote hain ki wo is se guzre (gone through it)."

Pichle chapter mein, humne seekha ki professors bahut hi detailed parameters kyun dete hain, khas taur par undergraduate program ke pehle do saalon mein. Aur itni detail ke bawajood bhi, students confuse (uljhan mein) aur uncertain (bina yakeen ke) hote hain. Unhe high school mein aise assignments nahi mile the. Wo janna chahte hain ki professor ko kaise khush (please) karna hai, isliye wo aur detail mangte hain. Yeh aise ache students hain jinka academic success ka ek record raha hai. Har acha student experience se yeh seekh chuka hota hai ki instructions ko follow karke hi ache grades milte hain.

Jab assignments ambiguous (uljhe hue) hote hain, toh students un strategies ka istemal nahi kar sakte jo unhone kindergarten se lekar high school tak istemal ki hain. Douglas Dowd (WashU) ne mujhe bataya ki ambiguity bahut zaroori (essential) hai, yahan tak ki yeh frustration (niraasha) ke level tak bhi ja sakti hai: "Yeh sab seekh (learning) ko ek experience mein daalne ke baare mein hai, jisme aam taur par frustration shamil hoti hai. Taaki jab tak knowledge haasil (gained) ho, yeh unki apni (owned) ban chuki ho. Yeh koi aisi cheez nahi hoti jo kisi ne aapko batayi ho." Agar wo kuch aesa kar rahe hain jiske sath wo pehle se hi comfortable hain, toh yeh ek problem hai kyunki wo seekh nahi rahe hain. Professor ka goal students ko theek us tarah se asahaj (uncomfortable) karna hai jahan sabse zyada seekh (learning) mil sake.

Kabhi-kabhi, students apni expectations (umeedon) ko saaf na karne ke liye teacher ko dosh (blame) dete hain. Professors is baat ke aadi (used to) hote hain aur wo students ko yeh samajhne mein madad karne ke liye taiyar (prepared) hote hain ki assignment ko aisa kyun hona padta hai, unhe is ambiguity ka samna kyun karna padta hai, aur yeh theek hai ki unhe sab kuch na pata ho. Ache professors ke paas students ko support karne ke liye empathy (humdardi), understanding (samajhdari), aur skill (hunar) hoti hai jab wo ambiguous assignments ko approach karne ke sath judi zaroori frustration ke zariye kaam karte hain.

Anther Kiley, ek graphic designer jinhone California Institute of the Arts aur Rhode Island School of Design mein padhaya hai, unhone mujhe us waqt ke baare mein bataya jab unhone pehli baar ek naya assignment padhaya jo unhone design kiya tha: students ko apna khud ka art show design karna tha. Wo pehle se hi ek experienced professor the, aur wo saalon ki teaching se jante the ki constraint aur ambiguity ke beech balance banana mushkil hai. Lekin yahan tak ki ek experienced instructor ke liye bhi, jab aap pehli baar koi naya assignment padhate hain, toh aap nahi jante ki yeh kaise kaam karega (work out). Is naye assignment ke sath, Kiley pareshan the ki unki instructions bahut ambiguous (uljhi hui) thin. Pehle hafte ke baad, unka darr sach hota hua lag raha tha: students ka kaam ek mess (bikhrav) tha. Lekin aakhir mein, assignment ki ambiguity ne students ko unke kuch sabse behtareen results ki taraf guide kiya.

*Yeh waqayi mushkil tha kyunki yeh itna open-ended tha aur isme itne saare alag-alag moving parts shamil the, jaise ek graphic pehchaan (identity) sochna, show ko curate (tariqa dain) karna, physical space (jagah) ko samajhna, yeh figure out (tay) karna ki physical space identity se kaise relate (judti) karti hai—in sab ko ek concept se juda (linked) hona tha jo puri cheez ko guide kare. Unka concept kya tha is par nirbhar (Depending) karte hue, isne har student ke liye ise navigate (rasta tay) karne ka ek alag tareeqa tay (dictated) kiya. Koi aesa saaf dhancha (clear structure) nahi ho sakta tha, misaal ke taur par, apni pehchaan (identity) se shuruat karein, kyunki shayad show ke concept ke hisab se iska koi matlab (sense) na banta. Ya aap space (jagah) se shuruat nahi kar sakte the, kyunki shayad kisi dusre student ke liye iska koi matlab na banta. Toh us assignment se hamesha bahut frustration (niraasha) hoti thi lekin aakhir mein isse waqayi, waqayi bahut ache results bhi nikal kar aate the [haste hain], bhale hi yeh students ke liye ek tarah ki museebat (misery) thi.*

Students sach mein dukhi (miserable) the, iske bawajood ki unhone shandaar (great) kaam generate kiya. "Kabhi-kabhi us waqt aur learning ke beech ek rishta (correspondence) hota hai jab kaam sabse dardnak (painful) ya sabse mushkil (challenging) hota hai, aur wahi par asal gehrai wali learning (rich learning) ho rahi hoti hai. Lekin kabhi-kabhi yeh batana mushkil hota hai ki students ke liye kaun sa dard (pain) acha hai aur kaun sa bura (not good pain)." Kiley ne iski tulna ek dusre assignment se ki jise wo istemal karte hain aur jo hamesha kafi aasani se (smoothly) nikal jata hai. Students ko yeh wala bahut pasand aata hai, aur Kiley jante the kyun: kyunki concepts students ke liye jaane-pehchane (familiar) the aur kyunki isme bahut kam ambiguity thi. "Main unhe yeh identity design project deta hoon, jise wo bahut pasand (love) karte hain. Wo is identity design project ko karna bahut pasand karte hain. Aur mujhe lagta hai iski wajah yeh hai ki unhe lagta hai, oh, humne identities dekhi hain. Humein pata hai ki hamein kya karna hai. Toh wo bas ek disha mein chal padte (barrel off) hain, aap jante hain? Aur phir mujhe ise ek tarah se todna (deconstruct) padta hai aur unhe apni sabhi sochon (assumptions) par sawal uthane par majboor karna padta hai aur raste mein bahut si cheezon ko bhulana (unlearn) padta hai."

Kiley ne aage (continued) apni baat batate hue poker (taash ka khel) ki misaal di jisme aap dikhate hain ki aapke paas kaun se patte (cards) hain. Unka matlab hai, students ke sath imandar (honest) rahein aur unhe hamesha batayein ki aapne assignment is tarah kyun design kiya hai. "Yeh bas creative process ki fitrat (nature) hai ki jab aap cheezon ko dhoondh (figure out) rahe hote hain toh isme ek faydemand ambiguity (productive ambiguity) ki haalat hoti hai. Aur yeh students ke liye na-gawar (unpleasant) ho sakta hai." Lekin wo jaan-bujh kar assignments aese design nahi karte ki students ko pareshani (frustrated) ho: "Main aese assignments dene mein interested nahi hoon jo apna poora patta (full hand) na dikhayen. Ya students ke liye aese frustrating problems set up karne mein jinhe unhe solve (work through) karna pade. Main unki izzat (respect) karta hoon aur main apna patta khol kar rakhna (lay out my hand) chahta hoon. Main unhe kisi khaas pal (moments) ki taraf le jane ki koshish nahi karta. Main cheezon ko chhipane (withhold) aur unhe dramai inkeshaf (dramatic reveal) ke palon ki taraf le jane ki koshish nahi karta, you know?"

Lekin kya kabhi-kabhi yeh behtar kaam nahi karta jab aap shuruat mein hi sab kuch nahi batate? Maine Kiley ko chapter 1 ke Tobi Kahn ke assignments ke baare mein bataya: pehle, students kisi bhi topic par painting karte hain; jab wo poora kar lete hain, toh wo unse kehte hain ki pehli painting mein se kisi ek element (hisse) ko chunkar dusri painting banayein aur pehli painting se ek alag kahani sunayein (tell a different story). Kahn ne kaha tha ki aap unhe pehle se nahi bata sakte ki dusra step "dusri painting mein ek alag kahani sunana" hoga kyunki yeh unke pehli painting ke baare mein sochne ke tareeqe ko badal dega. Agar students ko pehle se pata hoga ki aage kya aane wala hai toh yeh sikhane (pedagogically) ke lihaz se kaam nahi karega.

Kiley point (baat) samajh gaye. Unhone ek pal (moment) ke liye socha aur phir ek dilchasp (interesting) jawab diya:

*Khair, main unhe wese bhi (anyway) bata deta hoon, kyunki main janta hoon ki wo ise poori tarah se (fully) nahi samjhenge. Main yeh assignment sheets likhta hoon aur aakhir mein ek tips (mashware) ka section hota hai, aur main unhe tips padh kar sunata hoon, aur wo muskura rahe (smiling) hote hain aur haan mein sir hila rahe (nodding) hote hain, aur main janta hoon ki wo meri tips ko tab tak apne andar nahi bitthayenge (internalize) jab tak ki wo assignment ke us hisse par kaam karna shuru nahi kar dete.*

Apni poker ki misaal (metaphor) ka istemal karte hue, unhone "apna patta dikhaya (laid out his hand)," yeh jante hue ki students nahi samjhenge ki wo unhe kya bata rahe hain. Wo abhi tak ise nahi dekh sakte, bhale hi yeh unke theek samne ho. Aap student ko yeh nahi bata sakte ki unhe kya seekhne ki zaroorat hai jab tak ki wo ise seekhne ke liye taiyar (ready) na hon. Wo ise sirf practice (mashq) ke zariye hi seekh sakte hain. Steve DeFrank (SVA) ne ek kahani sunayi jiske liye har professor tarasta (lives for) hai:

*Mera ek student bahut fikarmand (concerned) ho gaya tha. Har hafte wo kehta tha, Steve, mujhe nahi pata aap mujhse kya chahte hain. Maine usse kaha, main bas itna chahta hoon ki tum kadi mehnat (work hard) karo. Jo tum kar rahe ho, wahi karo. Apna dil aur jaan (heart and soul) isme daal do aur iski fikar mat karo. Phir wo kehta tha, lekin nahi, aap kya chahte hain? Maine kaha, jab main ise dekhunga toh tumhe bata dunga. Aur semester ke aakhir mein, yeh waqayi bahut pyara (cute) tha, usne kaha, "Oh, main samajh gaya aap kya chahte hain. Aap chahte hain ki hum apne liye sochen (think for ourselves)." Maine kaha, "Haan (Yep)! Main yahi chahta hoon!"*

### **Mazbooti Se Date Rahein Jab Students Zyada Specifics Mangein (Hold Firm When Students Ask for More Specifics)**

Kyunki zyada-tar students ambiguity (uljhan) ke sath kaam karne ke aadi (used to) nahi hote, wo aksar instructor (teacher) se poochte hain ki kya wo assignment theek se (correctly) kar rahe hain. Lekin agar aap unhe ek specific (khaas) jawab dete hain, toh aap unhe problem ko approach (hal nikalne) karne aur solve karne ka apna rasta khud dhoondhne ke process se guzarne se rokte hain. Bahut se professors sawal ko unhi par wapas daal dete hain (turn the question back), aur unse jawab dene ko kehte hain, jaisa ki is chapter mein pehle Salinas aur DiGioia ne kiya tha.

Lekin Kiley (jankari) rokne (holding back) ke idea ke sath aaramdeh (comfortable) nahi hain; wo apne patte dikhane (revealing his hand), students se kuch na chhupane ki poker ki misaal ka istemal karte hain.

*Main bas uske sath comfortable nahi hoon. Mujhe yaad hai jab main ek student tha, kisi bhi aesi baat se mujhe bahut frustration (niraasha) hoti thi jahan faculty mujhe neecha dikhane ki koshish (patronizing) kar rahi hoti, ya yeh soch rahi hoti ki wo behtar jante hain, ya jaise wo mujhe dhokhe se (trick) kuch sikhane wale the, ya jaanbujh kar (intentionally) baaton ko gol-mol (cagey) ya chhipa kar (cryptic) rakhte. Mujhe lagta tha ki yeh hamesha ek aesi technique thi jise faculty is baat ko chhupane (cover) ke liye istemal karti thi [haste hain] ki wo poori tarah se taiyar (prepared) nahi the ya assignment par unki apni khud ki pakad (handle) theek nahi thi [haste hain]. Isliye main isse door (steer away) rehta hoon.*

Jab students sochte hain ki assignment bahut zyada ambiguous (uljha hua) hai aur wo pareshan (frustrated) ho jate hain, toh wo kabhi-kabhi instructor (teacher) par iska ilzam (blame) daalte hain ki unka padhane ka tareeqa asardaar nahi (ineffective pedagogy) hai. Students ko yeh samjhana zaroori hai ki niraasha (frustration) aur nakaami (failure) kyun zaroori (necessary) aur jaan-bujh kar ki gayi (intentional) hai; ambiguity ka matlab yeh nahi hai ki professor ne cheezon ko theek se plan nahi kiya. Lekin yahan tak ki jab aap unhe batate hain ki aap aesa kyun kar rahe hain, toh kabhi-kabhi students samajhne ke liye taiyar (ready) nahi hote. Aesi haalat mein, professors khud ko students se yeh kehta hua paate hain, "Tumhe abhi ke liye mujh par bharosa karna hoga (You have to trust me for right now)" (Claudia Hart, SAIC). Jab assignment poora ho jata hai, toh professor students se assignment ke bare mein baat karte hain (debriefs); ek baar jab wo assignment se guzar jate hain, toh wo yeh dekhne ke kabil ho jate hain ki professor ko shuru mein kuch jankari kyun chhupani (withhold) padi. Semester ke pehle assignment ke sath aesa karna khas taur par zaroori hai, taaki students jaan saken ki agli baar jab aesa hoga toh wo aap par vishwas (trust) kar sakte hain.

Amy Thompson, ek book artist jinka maine WashU mein interview liya, janti hain ki unke students ambiguity ka samna karte waqt ghabraye hue (nervous) hote hain. WashU desh ki top bees (20) universities mein se ek hai, aur wahan admission pana bahut competition (muqabla) bhara hai. Jo students admit hote hain unhe poori zindagi top grades (number) mile hote hain aur wo academic success ke liye be-tahasha committed (jazbati taur par jude) hote hain. "Wo waqayi yeh pakka (make sure) karna chahte hain ki wo assignment theek se (correctly) kar rahe hain. Lekin aksar kisi kaam ko karne ka koi ek sahi tareeqa (right way) *nahi* hota. Phir bhi, unke paas hamesha laakhon sawal (million questions) hote hain ki rules kya hain, aur wo kya kar sakte hain, aur wo kya nahi kar sakte hain. Mera jawab hota hai, 'Agar aapne mujhse yeh sawal na poocha hota aur khud yeh tay (decided) kiya hota ki jawab kya hai, toh aap kya karte?' Lekin unke paas waqayi kya na karne (what they can't do) ke baare mein koi sakht aur pakke niyam (hard and fast rules) nahi hain, main aise kisi niyam ko nahi manti (subscribe to)."

Jab students Thompson se poochte hain ki kya wo wo kar sakte hain jo unke dimaag (in mind) mein hai, "Toh yeh shayad koi aisi cheez hoti hai jo mujhe kabhi dimaag mein bhi nahi aayi (occurred) ki wo kabhi karna chahenge. Isliye mujhe waqayi hamesha nahi pata hota. Maine kabhi is baare mein socha hi nahi tha, ki kya main chahti thi ki wo aesa karein ya nahi. Isliye main yeh un par chhod deti hoon ki wo tay karein (decide) ki wo kya kar sakte hain aur kya nahi kar sakte."

### **Fixation se Bachna: Pichle Students ka Kaam Na Dikhayen (Avoiding Fixation: Don't Show Past Student Work)**

Students ko pichle students ka kaam dekhne ka idea bahut pasand hai, khas taur par un students ke projects dekhna jinhe bahut unche (high) grades mile the. Yeh is process ki uncertainty (ghair-yaqeeni surat-e-haal) ko behad kam (dramatically reduces) kar deta hai, kyunki students aage badhne ke tareeqe ke baare mein zyada mutmaeen (confident) mehsoos karte hain. Lekin yeh creative potential (sochne ki salahiyat) ko khatam kar deta hai aur thinking (soch) ko bandish (constrains) mein daal deta hai. Maine WashU mein ek illustrator, John Hendrix se poocha: Kya aap unhe pichle waqt (past) ke students ka kaam dikhate hain, aur kehte hain, "Yeh mat karo"? Aur phir unhe acha kaam bhi dikhate hain? Hendrix ne kaha,

*Yeh ek bahut hi bura (bad) idea hai. Aesa karne ki ek kashish (temptation) hoti hai aur maine isey aazmaya (tried) hai.
